fn header(
    file: &str,
    source: &str,
    from: &str,
    to: &str,
    prior: Option<&str>,
) -> Result<String, String> {
    if prior.is_some_and(|p| !word(p)) {
        return Err("qualified predecessor table origin needs an authored source".into());
    }
    let line = source.lines().next().ok_or("missing table header")?;
    let (tokens, _) = lexer::lex(file, line);
    let name = tokens
        .get(1)
        .filter(|t| t.kind == Kind::Word && t.value == from)
        .ok_or("missing table name span")?;
    let mut edits = vec![(
        offset(line, name.site.line, name.site.col),
        offset(line, name.site.line, name.site.col) + from.len(),
        to.to_owned(),
    )];
    if let Some(at) = tokens.iter().enumerate().skip(2).find_map(|(i,t)| (t.value=="renamed").then_some(i)) {
        let last = tokens
            .get(at + 2)
            .filter(|_| tokens.get(at + 1).is_some_and(|t| t.value == "from"))
            .ok_or("invalid table origin span")?;
        edits.push((
            offset(line, tokens[at].site.line, tokens[at].site.col),
            offset(line, last.site.line, last.site.col) + last.value.len(),
            prior
                .map(|p| format!("renamed from {p}"))
                .unwrap_or_default(),
        ));
    } else if let Some(prior) = prior {
        let colon = tokens
            .iter()
            .find(|t| t.value == ":")
            .ok_or("missing table header colon")?;
        let a = offset(line, colon.site.line, colon.site.col);
        edits.push((a, a, format!(" renamed from {prior}")));
    }
    let mut out = source.to_owned();
    edits.sort_by_key(|(a, _, _)| std::cmp::Reverse(*a));
    for (a, b, value) in edits {
        out.replace_range(a..b, &value);
    }
    Ok(out)
}
pub(super) fn arg_type(
    text: &str,
    tokens: &[lexer::Token],
    arg: &ast::ArgDecl,
) -> Result<(usize, usize), String> {
    let at = tokens
        .iter()
        .position(|t| t.site == arg.site)
        .ok_or("missing argument source span")?;
    let colon = (at..tokens.len())
        .find(|i| tokens[*i].value == ":")
        .ok_or("missing argument type colon")?;
    let start = tokens.get(colon + 1).ok_or("missing argument type")?;
    let mut depth = 0_i32;
    for (ordinal, t) in tokens.iter().skip(colon + 1).enumerate() {
        // `alias` and `default` are legal first nominal type tokens too.
        if ordinal > 0
            && depth == 0
            && (matches!(
                t.value.as_str(),
                "," | ")" | "=" | "default" | "alias" | "renamed" | "deprecated"
            ) || t.kind == Kind::Newline)
        {
            return Ok((
                offset(text, start.site.line, start.site.col),
                text[..offset(text, t.site.line, t.site.col)]
                    .trim_end()
                    .len(),
            ));
        }
        match t.value.as_str() {
            "(" | "[" | "<" => depth += 1,
            ")" | "]" | ">" => depth -= 1,
            _ => (),
        }
    }
    Err("unterminated argument type source span".into())
}
change the reachable effect without changing any copied guard. This applies
both to newly declared intent and intent already present in the predecessor.
Finite challenge campaigns are regression evidence, not an equivalence proof.

The presence of an invariant never makes ordinary schema evolution require a
view. The wave-25 replay exposed the Integration 23 regression in the earlier
"any invariant plus changed code" rule: it falsely refused ten of fifty v2
deploys, including simple renames and a split with a typed down. View registration
uses structural widening and old-row foresight facts, including hiding and
misrepresentation, rather than a general executable-digest change.

Final-existence obligations compare compiled constraints under injective declared
table and column identities, including typed reference keys and ordered plain-index
columns. Index labels and source spelling are not semantic changes; missing or
changed obligations still trigger registration. Literal values remain literal.

There is one repair protection independent of row-domain widening: behind
unchanged authored data/intent declarations and executable signatures, a changed
implementation with declared intent prepares a view. Signatures include module
identity and operation wire contracts; function/program bodies are excluded.
Thus a literal/helper-only policy repair or an executable-only worker/native
repair cannot restore the older executable behind unchanged intent. Identical
executable content remains a no-op; ordinary data/intent/contract evolution is
subject to the visibility trigger instead. Compiler-owned timeline event slots
are delivery metadata, not authored predecessor cells; retiring a rebuildable
feed alone does not require a rollback view. Ordinary authored fields in those
same source tables remain subject to the complete visibility check.

Preparation first attempts the **complete current authored source closure**,
including every declaration, schema domain, default, helper, program, job and
invariant. That strategy requires every predecessor table/column spelling and
operation name/kind and accepts every old argument under its wire name (including
exported aliases). An explicitly authored parameter alias may retain an old wire
name after a canonical rename. Every old spelling for one position must resolve
to the same current position, and distinct old positions must remain distinct.
Current result semantics remain a disclosed RESULT change. The same complete
closure is also a fallback for a guard/helper transport failure without intent.

If the strict complete-closure contract is unavailable, an intent-bearing view
uses a **guarded current workflow**. Its schema, defaults, helpers, programs,
shapes, jobs, aliases and independent intent remain the current authored source;
it never copies an old executable body or helper. A removed nullable,
default-free, non-engine, non-maintained predecessor field may remain absent in
this view instead of fabricating its value. The view reports that result change;
the old cell stays retained, and an old UI may need its usual missing-field
fallback. Required/defaulted predecessor fields and every predecessor table
still need a truthful compatible representation. A declared scalar or asset/reference
column identity rename may use its current spelling and current result semantics,
with an explicit old-UI result-change disclosure; this uses the compiler
validated identity mapping rather than inventing a predecessor value. The
same closed, capture-checked table alpha adapter may run before this
guarded-current strategy, so a nullable field gap does not discard a valid
declared table identity. Its current authority is compared under those identities
before strategy selection; an unsafe or unsupported alpha mapping still refuses.
No legacy schema field is introduced into current expression/name resolution.

Every predecessor query and every retained export must preserve its old accepted
wire inputs, aliases and distinct input positions. An unused parameter-alias slot
may retain an old spelling on the same current canonical argument; collisions,
rebinding, narrowing and unsupported mappings refuse. A missing old exported
alias may point only at an already authored compatible current endpoint.
Current explicit/rebound aliases retain their exact current target and guards.
A genuinely removed mutation, job or action may instead pause: its absence and
`E_OP` are named, and the author can supply a compatible current guarded endpoint
if that old workflow is still needed. Old code is never revived to make a removed
write available. Queries remain required so a view does not silently discard the
predecessor read surface. Writer/CAS, continuation and other activation gates
continue to apply to both strategies.

Without independent intent, the original selective guarded bridge remains
available. Its dependency checks resolve qualified helper calls to the same
validated global helper identity as the compiler, including transitive calls. Helper parameter types and row-reference AST slots
are compared under forward/inverse declared table identities; business strings,
member spellings and helper identities are never token-replaced.
A replaced or rebound exported alias or untransportable guard/dependency falls back to the
current workflow strategies above. Mixed-domain fallback uses the same compiled
qualified endpoint/alias contract; there is no unqualified source-text shortcut.
The selective predecessor workflow keeps its own accepted old wire contract;
current aliases cannot merge or rebind distinct old input positions behind
transported current guards. The complete-current and guarded-current strategies
above instead require the compiled current endpoint to accept every old input.
Guarded exports retain complete statement
order, query filters, helper calls and declared row identities; a differing ordered body or named-shape dependency
selects the current workflow instead of reconstructing authorization from the
last textual binding alone. Execution comparisons retain the complete AST,
including every merge lane prefix, source, loop domain, filter and projection;
only diagnostic source locations and validated row-identity spellings are
normalized. Human-readable expression printing is not a semantic signature.
Unrelated schema/domain additions are not guard
dependencies, and declared table identities are compared under their typed
renames rather than by raw spelling. Named shape definitions (including transitive
spreads) belong to that execution context; unchanged shape-use text does not
establish unchanged guard meaning. Field-authority mismatches participate in
strategy selection before the CLI admission gate, so a truthful guarded-current
view can be selected instead of refusing a valid deployment. Current shared-column defaults and authority are
checked after compilation.
A failed/capturing/ambiguous typed identity mapping cannot authorize fallback.
Source edits use original UTF-8 spans bounded by original file length even
without a final LF. Quota relaxation is structural registration input; tightening
on the same grouping is not. General constraint advice is not a second, less
precise registration predicate. Refusals identify the compatible guarded endpoint,
wire alias or truthful reverse projection to author. A hiding acknowledgment
never authorizes restoring an unsafe workflow, and ordinary security findings,
gates and runtime invariant boundaries still apply.

For this complete-current-closure path only, input-domain inclusion additionally
admits same-format text intervals whose current minimum is no larger and maximum
no smaller than the predecessor's. Identical domains, optional widening, enum
supersets and integer interval widening retain their existing meaning. New optional arguments may be omitted by old callers and take the current
language null value or their explicit authored default. Bounded lists admit recursive element-domain inclusion
only when current cardinality bounds contain the complete predecessor interval
and both element domains are declared (or both absent). Other formats, nominal
kinds and unsupported domains refuse.
This explicitly extends the initial closed rule above; it neither converts old
arguments nor broadens the separate selective guard/endpoint transport matcher.

The sole automatic spelling adapter is capture-avoiding alpha conversion of
explicit table identities in one source module. It rewrites typed table slots,
reference types and invariant subjects, retaining current bodies and literal
business strings. Every destination identity must reparse as a nominal reference
type, never a primitive scalar; argument aliases/defaults/deprecation metadata
remain outside replaced type spans. Mappings must be injective and cannot capture table, argument,
local-binding or shape names. Derived declarations, migrations, deliveries,
actions/effects, recursive/merge expressions and unsupported source forms require
an authored compatible source. The adapted closure recompiles and must satisfy
the same complete predecessor contract checks. Multi-module sources may use the
unchanged current closure directly; automatic cross-module renaming is not
claimed. No runtime policy, default, selection order or value conversion is
synthesized. Native/worker bundle requirements remain in force. Both predecessor and current
stage automatic/named worker dependencies are checked; a newly introduced
current worker cannot disappear from a generated view. The CLI additionally
compares complete executable digests, excluding registration/compiler/location
metadata: changing only worker/native bytes behind unchanged Quarry intent
triggers preparation and the same bundle boundary. Identical executable content
remains a no-op. Until complete immutable
bundles are captured and qualified, those generated views refuse.

For a pre-0.4.39 deployment without registered views, default rollback either
succeeds under writer-enforced visibility or refuses atomically with the logical
target and both recovery steps: `rollback <deployment> --to N --exact` (preview
its hiding first) and "redeploy with 0.4.39 to register a view". An equal-source
redeploy with an obsolete/missing view set still performs registration rather
than returning an unqualified UNCHANGED receipt.

### 20.2 Writer-enforced visibility

exec
/bin/bash -lc 'timeout 10s cat snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs/rollback_foresight.rs' in /home/ccheever/projects/snapback-sb4loop
 succeeded in 0ms:
//! @ref LLP 3000.000.000#16-release-reactivation-and-rollback-foresight-rel5 — future-write risks are distinct from snapshot validation.
use snapback4_core::{Schema, successor::{self, Change}, schema::ColumnDefault};
use std::path::Path;

pub(super) fn missing_defaults(candidate:&Schema,old:&Schema)->Result<Vec<String>,String>{
    let names=candidate.table_renames.iter().filter(|(new,prior)|old.table(prior).is_some()&&old.table(new).is_none()).map(|(new,prior)|(prior.clone(),new.clone())).collect();
    let old=old.with_table_names(&names).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let mut missing=Vec::new();
    for(name,target)in old.visible_tables().filter(|(_,t)|t.ephemeral.is_none()){
        let Some(from)=candidate.table(name)else{continue};if old.maintain_for(name).is_some(){continue}
        for(column,kind)in &target.columns {
            let renamed=candidate.asset_renames.get(name).is_some_and(|map|map.iter().any(|(new,prior)|prior==column&&from.columns.contains_key(new)));
            if !target.engine_field(column)&&!snapback4_core::schema::timeline::is_slot(column)&&!target.maintained.contains_key(column)&&matches!(target.defaults.get(column),Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(_)))&&!from.columns.contains_key(column)&&!renamed&&!projected(candidate,name,column,kind){missing.push(format!("{name}.{column}"));}
        }
    }Ok(missing)
}

pub(super) fn risks(candidate: &Schema, old: &Schema, release: u64, dir: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
    risks_for(candidate,old,release,dir,true,true)
}
// Timeline event slots are compiler-owned delivery metadata, not authored old
// cells. Retiring a rebuildable feed must not manufacture a visibility view.
pub(super) fn old_row_risks(candidate:&Schema,old:&Schema,release:u64,dir:&Path)->Vec<String>{
    risks_for(candidate,old,release,dir,false,false)
}
fn risks_for(candidate:&Schema,old:&Schema,release:u64,dir:&Path,include_new_tables:bool,include_constraints:bool)->Vec<String>{
    let names:std::collections::BTreeMap<_,_>=candidate.table_renames.iter().filter(|(new,prior)|old.table(prior).is_some() && old.table(new).is_none()).map(|(new,prior)|(prior.clone(),new.clone())).collect();
    if names.is_empty(){return risks_aligned(candidate,old,release,dir,include_new_tables,include_constraints)}
    let aligned=match old.with_table_names(&names){Ok(schema)=>schema,Err(error)=>return vec![format!("rollback foresight cannot compare renamed identities: {error}; run exact rollback --check")]};
    risks_aligned(candidate,&aligned,release,dir,include_new_tables,include_constraints).into_iter().map(|line|{
        let (mut facts,command)=line.split_once("; run `").map_or((line.clone(),String::new()),|(facts,command)|(facts.to_owned(),format!("; run `{command}")));
        for (prior,new) in &names {facts=facts.replace(&format!(", {new}."),&format!(", {prior} (current {new}).")).replace(&format!("table {new}:"),&format!("table {prior} (current {new}):")).replace(&format!("unique index {new}."),&format!("unique index {prior} (current {new})."));}
        facts+&command
    }).collect()
}
fn risks_aligned(candidate:&Schema,old:&Schema,release:u64,dir:&Path,include_new_tables:bool,include_constraints:bool)->Vec<String>{
    let mut risks:Vec<_>=successor::classify(candidate,old).into_iter().filter_map(|step| {
        let path=step.path.strip_prefix("tables.")?;
        let (table,column)=path.split_once(".columns.")?;
        let target=old.tables.get(table)?;
        if target.engine_field(column) || snapback4_core::schema::timeline::is_slot(column) || old.maintain_for(table).is_some() || target.maintained.contains_key(column) {return None;}
        let before=target.columns.get(column)?;
        if projected(candidate,table,column,before) {return None;}

        let from=candidate.tables.get(table)?;
        let source_kind=from.columns.get(column).or_else(||candidate.asset_renames.get(table)?.iter().find(|(_,old)|old.as_str()==column).and_then(|(new,_)|from.columns.get(new)));
        let reason=match source_kind {
            None if !before.is_optional() && !matches!(target.defaults.get(column),Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(_))) =>
                "without a fitting reverse projection, new rows will lack the old required field, so rollback can refuse".to_owned(),
            Some(kind) if kind!=before && matches!(step.change,Change::Narrowing|Change::Destructive) =>
                format!("new writes allowed by {kind} may not fit {before}, so rollback can hide rows; {}", remedy(before)),
            _ => return None,
        };
        Some(format!("rollback foresight: release {release}, {table}.{column}: {reason}; keep an expand/contract bridge with the old required fields and compatible domains; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()))
    }).collect();
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#19-dormant-visibility-and-migration-dorm — warn before new writes, including harmless newer-only facts and empty legacy cells.
    for (name, _) in candidate.visible_tables().filter(|(name,t)|t.ephemeral.is_none() && candidate.maintain_for(name).is_none()) {
        if include_new_tables && old.table(name).is_none() && !candidate.table_renames.get(name).is_some_and(|source|old.table(source).is_some()) {
            risks.push(format!("rollback foresight: rows written to new table {name} will be hidden while release {release} is live; release {release} has no such table, so these are newer-only facts, not lost old-table records; a same-table `down` cannot create this missing table; keep it in a loosened bridge or declare a table rename; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()));
        }
    }
    for (name,target) in old.visible_tables().filter(|(_,t)|t.ephemeral.is_none()) {
        let Some(from)=candidate.tables.get(name) else {continue;};
        if old.maintain_for(name).is_some() {continue;}
        for (column,kind) in &target.columns {
            if projected(candidate,name,column,kind) || from.columns.contains_key(column) || target.engine_field(column) || snapback4_core::schema::timeline::is_slot(column) || target.maintained.contains_key(column) {continue;}
            let interpretation=if kind.is_optional() {"null".to_owned()} else if let Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(value))=target.defaults.get(column) {format!("the old default {}",snapback4_core::wire::to_json(value))} else {continue;};
            let suggestions=cross_table_suggestions(candidate,old,name,column,kind,target.defaults.get(column));
            risks.push(format!("rollback foresight: release {release}, {name}.{column}: without a fitting reverse projection, records created while this field is absent have no retained old cell and will show {interpretation} to release {release} users; declare a reverse projection from the replacement value, or retain the field in a bridge release; {suggestions}run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()));
        }
        // Registration compares constraint domains structurally in
        // release_bridge::requires_view (including renamed fields). General
        // foresight advice also explains constraint changes, but is not a
        // second, less precise admission predicate.
        if include_constraints {
        for (index,definition) in &target.indexes {
            if definition.unique && from.indexes.get(index)!=Some(definition) {
                risks.push(format!("rollback foresight: release {release}, unique index {name}.{index}: future writes may introduce duplicate keys and refuse restoration; keep the old uniqueness in a bridge; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()));
            }
        }
        if target.constraints.iter().any(|constraint| {
            if from.constraints.contains(constraint){return false;}
            match constraint {
                snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Text{field,..}=>!target.columns.get(field).is_some_and(|kind|projected(candidate,name,field,kind)),
                snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Cap{by,max}=>!from.constraints.iter().any(|c|matches!(c,snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::Cap{by:next_by,max:next_max}if next_by==by&&next_max<=max)),
                snapback4_core::schema::Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{..}=>true,
                _=>false,
            }
        }) {
            risks.push(format!("rollback foresight: release {release}, table {name}: changed constraints may let future writes refuse rollback; preserve old bounds/quotas in a bridge; run `snapback4 rollback {} --to {release} --check --bridge <new-dir>` after writes",dir.display()));
        }
        }
    }
    risks
}

fn projected(candidate:&Schema,table:&str,column:&str,kind:&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType)->bool {
    candidate.extractions.iter().any(|e|e.parent==table&&e.fields.values().any(|c|c==column)) || (scalar(kind) && candidate.downfills.iter().any(|f|f.table==table && f.outputs.contains(column))) || candidate.asset_renames.get(table).is_some_and(|map|map.iter().any(|(new,old)|old==column && candidate.tables.get(table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(new))==Some(kind)))
}
fn scalar(kind:&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType)->bool {
    use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::*;
    match kind {Optional(k)=>scalar(k),Image{..}|Video{..}|File{..}|List{..}|ListRange{..}|Ref{..}=>false,_=>true}
}
fn remedy(kind:&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType)->&'static str {
    if scalar(kind) {"declare a truthful scalar `down`, or generate a loosened bridge"}
    else {"computed `down` cannot project files, images, collections or references; generate a loosened bridge, or keep the required value and add a status"}
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    fn schema(source:&str)->Schema {
        let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);b.schema
    }
    #[test]
    fn warns_before_any_future_rows_exist_but_compatible_additions_are_quiet() {
        let base="table items:\n  required: int 0..10\n  state: enum('old')\n  public\n";
        let old=schema(base);
        for (candidate,subject) in [
            (base.replace("  required: int 0..10\n",""),"items.required"),
            (base.replace("int 0..10","int 0..20"),"items.required"),
            (base.replace("enum('old')","enum('old','new')"),"items.state"),
            (base.replace("int 0..10","int 0..10 ?"),"items.required"),
        ] {
            let warnings=risks(&schema(&candidate),&old,1,Path::new("deployment"));
            assert!(warnings.iter().any(|w|w.contains(subject)&&w.contains("--to 1 --check --bridge")),"{warnings:?}");
        }
        assert!(risks(&schema(&base.replace("  public","  label: text <=20 ?\n  public")),&old,1,Path::new("deployment")).is_empty());
        let unique=schema(&base.replace("  public","  unique state\n  public"));
        assert!(risks(&old,&unique,1,Path::new("deployment")).iter().any(|w|w.contains("duplicate keys")));
    }
    #[test]
    fn warns_about_newer_only_tables_and_empty_legacy_cells_before_any_writes() {
        let old=schema("table items:\n  reason: text <=40 ?\n  public\n");
        let next=schema("table items:\n  public\ntable explanations:\n  text: text <=40\n  public\n");
        let warnings=risks(&next,&old,1,Path::new("deployment"));
        assert!(warnings.iter().any(|w|w.contains("new table explanations") && w.contains("newer-only facts")),"{warnings:?}");
        assert!(warnings.iter().any(|w|w.contains("items.reason") && w.contains("show null") && w.contains("reverse projection")),"{warnings:?}");
    }
    #[test]
    fn declared_down_and_scalar_or_asset_renames_do_not_warn_about_absent_fields() {
        let old=schema("table items:\n  state: enum('old')\n  public\n");
        let new=schema("table items:\n  state: enum('old','new')\n  public\ndown items oldState(state: enum('old','new')): {state:'old'}\n");
        assert!(risks(&new,&old,1,Path::new("deployment")).is_empty());
        for kind in ["text <=40 ?","file <=1024 ?"] {
            let old=schema(&format!("table items:\n  title: {kind}\n  public\n"));
            let new=schema(&format!("table items:\n  name: {kind} renamed from title\n  public\n"));
            assert!(risks(&new,&old,1,Path::new("deployment")).is_empty(),"{kind}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn table_rename_still_warns_about_a_loosened_required_old_field() {
        let old=schema("table orders:\n  n: int\n  public\n");
        let new=schema("table records renamed from orders:\n  n: int ?\n  public\n");
        let warnings=risks(&new,&old,1,Path::new("deployment"));
        assert!(warnings.iter().any(|w|w.contains("orders (current records).n")&&w.contains("hide rows")),"{warnings:?}");
        assert!(!warnings.iter().any(|w|w.contains("new table records")),"{warnings:?}");
    }

}

fn cross_table_suggestions(candidate:&Schema,old:&Schema,parent:&str,column:&str,kind:&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType,default:Option<&ColumnDefault>)->String {
    let fallback=match default {Some(ColumnDefault::Literal(v))=>match v{snapback4_core::Value::Bool(v)=>v.to_string(),snapback4_core::Value::Int(v)=>v.to_string(),snapback4_core::Value::String(v)=>snapback4_lang::print::quote(v),_=>return String::new()},_ if kind.is_optional()=>"null".into(),_=>return String::new()};
    let mut proposals=Vec::new();
    for(child,table)in candidate.visible_tables().filter(|(name,_)|old.table(name).is_none()) {
        for(reference,ty)in &table.columns {
            if !matches!(ty.required(),snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Ref{table} if table==parent){continue}
            let values=table.columns.iter().filter(|(c,k)|!table.engine_field(c)&&c.as_str()!=reference.as_str()&&k.required()==kind.required()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            let unique=table.indexes.values().any(|i|i.unique&&i.columns()==vec![reference.clone()]);
            if unique&&values.len()==1 {
                let value=values[0].0;
                proposals.push(format!("candidate cross-table down (confirm the replacement field's meaning): `down {parent} rollback{column}(id: id): {{{column}: {child}[{reference} = id].{value} ?? {fallback}}}`"));
            }else if kind.required()==&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Bool {
                proposals.push(format!("if child existence exactly means {column}, candidate cross-table down: `down {parent} rollback{column}(id: id): {{{column}: exists {child} where .{reference} = id}}`"));
            }
        }
    }
    if proposals.is_empty(){String::new()}else{proposals.join("; ")+"; "}
}

"""EV-1 public release witness, using the serving harness's owned cluster/children."""
# @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#3.5 — configured deploy/serve and a store-independent wire comparison.
import json, time

V1 = '''use identity
table notes:
  n: int
  memo: json <=100 ?
  site: text <=200 format url ?
  by byN: n, id
  public
  insert <- allow
  update <- allow
  online only
table retired:
  text: text <=100
  public
  insert <- allow
  online only
mutation put(key: id, n: int, memo: json <=100 ?, site: text <=200 format url ?):
  upsert notes[key] {n, memo, site}
  return n
mutation archive(key: id, text: text <=100):
  insert retired {id: key, text}
  return text
query peek(key: id):
  return notes[key]
query archived(key: id):
  return retired[key]
query ordered():
  return notes first 10 by byN
'''
V2 = '''use identity
table notes:
  n: int
  extra: text <=100 ?
  count: int default 7
  by byCount: count, id
  public
  insert <- allow
  update <- allow
  online only
table added:
  n: int
  public
  insert <- allow
  online only
mutation put(key: id, n: int):
  upsert notes[key] {n}
  return n
mutation add(key: id, n: int):
  insert added {id: key, n}
  return n
query peek(key: id):
  return notes[key]
query addedRow(key: id):
  return added[key]
query ordered():
  return notes where .count = 7 first 10 by byCount
'''

V3 = V1.replace('  memo: json <=100 ?\n', '  memo: json <=100 ?\n  extra: text <=100 ?\n  count: int default 7\n')

def witness(h, pg_url):
    work, cli, start, stop, http = (h[k] for k in ('WORK','cli','start','stop','http'))
    transcripts = {}
    for backend, target in [('postgres', pg_url), ('sqlite', None)]:
        deployment = work / ('ev1-' + backend)
        records = []
        assert V3 != V1 and 'format url ?' in V3
        for version, source in [(1,V1),(2,V2),(3,V3)]:
            (work/'app/snapback/schema.q').write_text(source)
            args = ['deploy', str(deployment)]
            if version == 3: args += ['--restore-dormant']
            cli(args, url=target)
            child, base = start('ev1-'+backend+'-v'+str(version),target,deployment=deployment)
            guest = http(base,'POST','/auth/guest',{})['session']
            token = guest['token']
            state = http(base,'GET','/schema',token=token)
            assert state['generation']==version,state
            def capture(label, value):
                # Each backend mints its own guest principal. Check its value
                # before excluding that nondeterministic identity from comparison.
                if ' q ' in label: assert value['viewer']==guest['principal'],value
                if 'store_id' in value: assert value['store_id']==state['store_id'],value
                independent = {k:v for k,v in value.items() if k not in ('store_id','now','viewer')}
                records.append((label,json.dumps(independent,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':'))))
                return value
            def mutate(name, identity, args):
                return capture(f'v{version} m {name}',http(base,'POST','/m/'+name,{'id':identity,'args':args,'store_id':state['store_id']},token))
            def query(name, args):
                return capture(f'v{version} q {name}',http(base,'POST','/q/'+name,{'args':args},token))
            if version == 1:
                assert mutate('put','ev1-a',{'key':'a','n':2,'memo':'雪\0exact','site':'https://example.com'})['state']=='sent'
                assert mutate('put','ev1-b',{'key':'b','n':1,'memo':None,'site':None})['state']=='sent'
                assert mutate('archive','ev1-z',{'key':'z','text':'retained row'})['state']=='sent'
                first = query('peek',{'key':'a'})['data']
                archived = query('archived',{'key':'z'})['data']
                assert [r['n'] for r in query('ordered',{})['data']]==[1,2]
            elif version == 2:
                changed = query('peek',{'key':'a'})['data']
                assert changed == {'id':'a','n':2,'extra':None,'count':7},changed
                assert len(query('ordered',{})['data'])==2
                assert mutate('add','ev1-new',{'key':'new','n':9})['state']=='sent'
                assert query('addedRow',{'key':'new'})['data']['n']==9
            else:
                assert query('peek',{'key':'a'})['data']==dict(first,extra=None,count=7)
                assert query('archived',{'key':'z'})['data']==archived
                assert [r['n'] for r in query('ordered',{})['data']]==[1,2]
            stop(child)
            if backend == 'postgres':
                deadline=time.monotonic()+15
                while True:
                    doctor=json.loads(cli(['doctor','--release',str(deployment),'--json'],url=target).stdout)
                    if doctor['store']['expired']: break
                    assert time.monotonic()<deadline,doctor
                    time.sleep(.1)
        transcripts[backend]=json.dumps(records,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')).encode()
        (work/('ev1-'+backend+'-wire.json')).write_bytes(transcripts[backend])
        print('P1-EV-1',backend,'PASS: v1 writes, v2 additions/retirements/indexes, v3 exact restoration',flush=True)
    assert transcripts['postgres']==transcripts['sqlite'], 'EV-1 wire bytes differ'
    (work/'app/snapback/schema.q').write_text(h['source'])
    print('P1-EV-1 PASS: byte-identical wire after removing store_id, now and the checked minted guest viewer',flush=True)
    large_default_witness(h, pg_url.replace('schema=ev1', 'schema=ev1_large'))


def large_default_witness(h, pg_url):
    """A restored raw parent image must widen /changes identically on both stores."""
    work, cli, start, stop, http = (h[k] for k in ('WORK','cli','start','stop','http'))
    v1 = '''use identity
table notes:
  n: int
  memo: json <=100 ?
  public
  insert <- allow
  delete <- allow
  online only
table children:
  note: notes on delete restrict
  public
  insert <- allow
  online only
mutation put(key: id):
  insert notes {id: key, n: 1}
mutation erase(key: id):
  delete notes[key]
query peek(key: id):
  return notes[key]
'''
    sibling = "  payload: text <=307200 default '" + 'x' * (300 * 1024) + "'\n"
    versions = [v1, v1.replace('  memo: json <=100 ?\n', sibling),
        v1.replace('  memo: json <=100 ?\n', '  memo: json <=100 ?\n' + sibling)]
    answers = {}
    for backend, target in [('postgres', pg_url), ('sqlite', None)]:
        deployment = work / ('ev1-large-' + backend)
        for version, source in enumerate(versions, 1):
            (work/'app/snapback/schema.q').write_text(source)
            cli(['deploy', str(deployment)] + (['--restore-dormant'] if version == 3 else []), url=target)
            child, base = start(f'ev1-large-{backend}-v{version}', target, deployment=deployment)
            guest = http(base, 'POST', '/auth/guest', {})['session']
            token = guest['token']
            state = http(base, 'GET', '/schema', token=token)
            assert state['generation'] == version, state
            def mutate(name):
                return http(base, 'POST', '/m/'+name, {'id':f'large-{name}',
                    'args':{'key':'a'}, 'store_id':state['store_id']}, token)
            if version == 1:
                assert mutate('put')['state'] == 'sent'
            else:
                row = http(base, 'POST', '/q/peek', {'args':{'key':'a'}}, token)
                assert row['data']['payload'] == 'x' * (300 * 1024)
                if version == 3:
                    assert row['data']['memo'] is None
                    assert mutate('erase')['state'] == 'sent'
                    answer = http(base, 'GET', f"/changes?since={row['seq']}&wait=0", token=token)
                    answers[backend] = {k:v for k,v in answer.items() if k not in ('store_id','now')}
                    print('P1-EV-1 300 KiB', backend, '/changes:', answers[backend], flush=True)
            stop(child)
            if backend == 'postgres':
                deadline = time.monotonic() + 15
                while True:
                    doctor = json.loads(cli(['doctor','--release',str(deployment),'--json'],url=target).stdout)
                    if doctor['store']['expired']: break
                    assert time.monotonic() < deadline, doctor
                    time.sleep(.1)
    assert answers['sqlite']['changes'] is None, answers
    assert answers['postgres'] == answers['sqlite'], '300 KiB restored parent /changes differs'
    (work/'ev1-large-changes.json').write_text(json.dumps(answers, sort_keys=True))
    (work/'app/snapback/schema.q').write_text(h['source'])
    print('P1-EV-1 PASS: 300 KiB restored parent /changes is null on both stores', flush=True)

//! Mutable syntax traversal shared by planning and final index-name resolution.
use crate::ast::*;

pub(crate) fn expression(value: &mut Expr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    match value {
        Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => read_children(read, visit),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. } | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { expression(left, visit); expression(right, visit); }
        Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => { for item in items { expression(item, visit); } }
        Expr::Object { fields, .. } => { for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } } }
        Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => { expression(base, visit); shape(fields, visit); }
        Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => { expression(then, visit); expression(condition, visit); expression(otherwise, visit); }
        Expr::Walk { seed, .. } => expression(seed, visit),
        Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => {
            for key in keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
            for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } }
            if let Some(value) = condition { expression(value, visit); }
        }
        Expr::Merge(merge) => {
            for value in [&mut merge.matching, &mut merge.filter, &mut merge.cursor].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
            let mut lanes = Vec::new();
            for mut lane in std::mem::take(&mut merge.lanes) {
                if let Some(predicate) = &mut lane.admission { expression(predicate, visit); }
                if let Some((_, read)) = &mut lane.nulls {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    let Expr::Read(planned) = value else { unreachable!("planned null scan") };
                    **read = planned;
                }
                if let Some(value) = &mut lane.distinct { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(fields) = &mut lane.project { shape(fields, visit); }
                for value in &mut lane.prefix { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some((_, value)) = &mut lane.loop_ { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(read) = &mut lane.planned {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    match value {
                        Expr::Read(planned) => **read = planned,
                        Expr::Merge(nested) if lane.loop_.is_none() => {
                            for mut child in nested.lanes {
                                child.branch = lane.branch.clone();
                                child.project = lane.project.clone();
                                lanes.push(child);
                            }
                            continue;
                        }
                        _ => unreachable!("a planned lane is a read or an expanded read"),
                    }
                }
                lanes.push(lane);
            }
            merge.lanes = lanes;
        }
        Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => {}
    }
    visit(value);
}

fn read_children(read: &mut ReadExpr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for key in &mut read.keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
    for seek in &mut read.seeks { expression(&mut seek.value, visit); }
    for value in [&mut read.filter, &mut read.matching].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
    if let Some(value) = read.range.as_mut().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_mut()) { expression(value, visit); }
}

pub(super) fn shape(fields: &mut [ShapeField], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for field in fields { if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { expression(value, visit); } }
}

pub(super) fn statements(body: &mut [Stmt], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for stmt in body {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Bind { value, .. } | Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expression(condition, visit),
            Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                expression(payload, visit);
                if let Some(key) = once { expression(key, visit); }
            }
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => { expression(condition, visit); statements(then_body, visit); statements(else_body, visit); }
            Stmt::As { principal: domain, body, .. } | Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => { expression(domain, visit); statements(body, visit); }
            Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => {}
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn document(doc: &mut Document, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#8-wave-21-release-evolution — historical lookups receive the same predicate/index planning as ordinary reads.
    for fill in doc.backfills.iter_mut().chain(&mut doc.downfills) {expression(&mut fill.value,visit);}

    for program in &mut doc.programs { statements(&mut program.prelude, visit); statements(&mut program.body, visit); }
    for function in &mut doc.functions {
        match &mut function.body { FunctionBody::Expression(value) => expression(value, visit), FunctionBody::Block(body) => statements(body, visit) }
    }
    for decl in &mut doc.shapes { shape(&mut decl.fields, visit); }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
pub enum LeavePolicy {
    #[default]
    UntilRevoked,
    DeliveredHistory,
}

/// `sync public`, `sync to .column`, or `sync to target[.prefix, ...]`, each
/// with an optional horizon `last N by index` (LLP 2000.000 §7.1, LLP 3000 §7).
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Sync {
    /// The group arm when the table has one, else the first column arm.
    pub audience: Audience,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub horizon: Option<Horizon>,
    /// A union's further arms, principal columns only: a device holds a
    /// row while any arm admits its viewer (LLP 3000.036.000.000 §2.3).
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
    pub also: Vec<SyncArm>,
}

/// One further arm of a union audience. Its legacy window field is refused at admission.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct SyncArm {
    pub audience: Audience,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub horizon: Option<Horizon>,
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Audience {
    /// Every principal holds the same partition.
    Public,
    /// The principal named by this row's column.
    Column(String),
    /// Every principal with a row in `table` whose `prefix` columns equal this
    /// row's `prefix` columns, read from the target's `principal` column.
    Target { table: String, prefix: Vec<String>, principal: String },
}

/// `last N by index`: a device holds at most N rows per group, where the group
/// is the audience prefix, in the index's descending order.
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Horizon {
    pub last: u32,
    pub by: String,
}

impl Audience {
    /// The content columns that name this audience's group: none for the
    /// public, the column for a column audience, the prefix for a target.
    pub fn columns(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        match self {
            Audience::Public => Vec::new(),
            Audience::Column(column) => vec![column.clone()],
            Audience::Target { prefix, .. } => prefix.clone(),
        }
    }

pub fn is_int_floor(min: &i64) -> bool { *min <= int_floor() }
pub fn is_int_ceiling(max: &i64) -> bool { *max >= int_ceiling() }
/// An int range some i53 value satisfies, with both ends inside i53.
pub fn int_range_valid(min: i64, max: i64) -> bool { int_floor() <= min && min <= max && max <= int_ceiling() }
/// A stored end as written: `None` when it is the i53 limit.
pub fn int_bound(end: i64, limit: i64) -> Option<i64> { (end != limit).then_some(end) }

/// Whether `value` lies inside an inclusive optional range.
pub fn int_in_range(value: i64, min: Option<i64>, max: Option<i64>) -> bool {
    min.is_none_or(|min| value >= min) && max.is_none_or(|max| value <= max)
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Index {
    pub components: Vec<IndexComponent>,
    pub unique: bool,
}

impl Index {
    pub fn of(columns: &[&str], unique: bool) -> Self {
        Self { components: columns.iter().map(|c| IndexComponent::Column((*c).to_owned())).collect(), unique }
    }

    /// Ordered key names: columns and time-function names. A `words(..)`
    /// component contributes nothing here; `word_component` names it.
    /// Use `IndexComponent::column` for actual row fields (such as rule pins).
    pub fn columns(&self) -> Vec<String> {
        self.components.iter().filter_map(|c| c.key_name().cloned()).collect()
    }

    pub fn is_plain(&self) -> bool {
        self.components.iter().all(|c| matches!(c, IndexComponent::Column(_)))
    }

    pub fn uses_column(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
        self.components.iter().any(|component| match component {
            IndexComponent::Column(column) => column == name,
            IndexComponent::Calendar(key) => key.column == name,
            IndexComponent::WordPrefixes { fields, .. } => fields.iter().any(|field| field == name),
            IndexComponent::Contribution(maintain) => maintain.dependencies().contains(name),
        })
    }

    /// The `words(..)` component, if any: its position among the
    /// components and the text columns it tokenizes.
    pub fn word_component(&self) -> Option<(usize, &[String])> {
        self.components.iter().enumerate().find_map(|(position, component)| match component {
            IndexComponent::WordPrefixes { fields, .. } => Some((position, fields.as_slice())),
            IndexComponent::Column(_) | IndexComponent::Calendar(_) | IndexComponent::Contribution(_) => None,
        })
    }

    /// The columns after the first `components` components: a cursor's
    /// suffix, and the order a page continues in. Counted in components,
    /// so a scan whose prefix ends with a word skips the word too.
    pub fn suffix_after(&self, components: usize) -> Vec<String> {
        self.components.iter().skip(components).filter_map(|c| c.key_name().cloned()).collect()
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum IndexComponent {
    /// Internal selected physical source rows; an optional following expiry component
    /// orders the group by deadline (null last), then the appended source id.
    Contribution(Box<Maintain>),
    Column(String),
    Calendar(crate::maintain::CalendarKey),
    WordPrefixes { fields: Vec<String>, #[serde(default)] contract: serde_json::Value },
}

impl IndexComponent {
    pub fn key_name(&self) -> Option<&String> {
        match self { Self::Calendar(key) => Some(&key.function), _ => self.column() }
    }
    pub fn key_type(&self, table: &Table) -> Option<ColumnType> {
        match self { Self::Calendar(_) => Some(ColumnType::Int), Self::Column(name) => table.columns.get(name).cloned(), _ => None }
    }
    pub fn value(&self, row: &Value) -> Option<Value> {
        match self { Self::Calendar(key) => Some(key.value(row)), Self::Column(name) => Some(row.field(name).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null)), _ => None }
    }
    pub fn column(&self) -> Option<&String> {
        match self {
            Self::Column(column) => Some(column),
            Self::Calendar(_) | Self::WordPrefixes { .. } | Self::Contribution(_) => None,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct Rules {
    pub read: RuleExpr,
    pub insert: RuleExpr,
    pub update: RuleExpr,
    pub delete: RuleExpr,
}

impl Rules {
    pub fn deny() -> Self {
        Self { read: RuleExpr::Deny, insert: RuleExpr::Deny, update: RuleExpr::Deny, delete: RuleExpr::Deny }
    }

    pub fn for_effect(&self, effect: Effect) -> &RuleExpr {
        match effect {
            Effect::Read => &self.read,
            Effect::Insert => &self.insert,
            Effect::Update => &self.update,
            Effect::Delete => &self.delete,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Effect {
    Read,
    Insert,
    Update,
    Delete,
}

impl fmt::Display for Effect {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(match self {
            Self::Read => "read",
            Self::Insert => "insert",
            Self::Update => "update",
            Self::Delete => "delete",
            Self::Delete => "delete",
        })
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleExpr {
    Public { reason: String },
    Allow,
    Deny,
    Eq(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    Ne(RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    /// Ordered comparisons are confined to named update transitions and to
    /// insert rules over the row's own required int/time columns.
    Compare(crate::ir::BinaryOp, RuleTerm, RuleTerm),
    And(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Or(Vec<RuleExpr>),
    Not(Box<RuleExpr>),
    Exists {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
    /// True only when the indexed target row was inserted earlier in this
    /// same transaction; insert rules only.
    Created {
        table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm>, provenance: KeyProvenance,
        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
        equalities: Vec<(String, RuleTerm)>,
    },
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn is_public(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. })
    }

    pub fn is_uniformly_readable(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow)
    }

    /// Every `.field` the rule reads, on any probe key or comparison.
    pub fn fields(&self, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>| {
            if let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::Reference { column: field, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column: field, .. } = term {
                out.insert(field.clone());
            }
        };
        match self {
            Self::Public { .. } | Self::Allow | Self::Deny => {}
            Self::Eq(left, right) | Self::Ne(left, right) | Self::Compare(_, left, right) => {
                term(left, out);
                term(right, out);
            }
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| item.fields(out)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.fields(out),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => {
                key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_, term)| term)).for_each(|item| term(item, out));
            }
        }
    }

    /// The disjuncts of the rule: an `or` flattens, anything else is itself.
    pub fn disjuncts(&self) -> Vec<&RuleExpr> {
        match self {
            Self::Or(items) => items.iter().flat_map(|item| item.disjuncts()).collect(),
            other => vec![other],
        }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.6 — service seats read on the server and never enlarge a device's audience.
            Self::And(items) => items.iter().map(|i| if matches!(i,Self::Or(_)){format!("({})",i.quarry())}else{i.quarry()}).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and "),
            Self::Or(items) => items.iter().map(|i| i.quarry()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or "),
            Self::Not(inner) => format!("not ({})", inner.quarry()),
            Self::Exists { table, key, equalities, .. } => format!("exists {table}[{}]", key.iter().map(term).chain(equalities.iter().map(|(col, value)| format!("{col} = {}", term(value)))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
            Self::Created { table, key, equalities, .. } => format!("created {table}[{}]", key.iter().map(term).chain(equalities.iter().map(|(col, value)| format!("{col} = {}", term(value)))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        }
    }
}

pub use reference::{RuleTerm, RuleHop, MAX_RULE_HOPS};

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Constraint {
    /// Shared decoder fence. Only the schema codec writes this wire carrier.
    Capabilities(BTreeSet<String>),
    /// Decode-only marker from unpublished F2 stores; the schema codec removes it.
    #[serde(skip_serializing)]
    Recorded,
    Unique { index: String },
    Cap { by: Vec<String>, max: u32 },
    AppendOnly,
    Immutable { fields: Vec<String> },
    Text { field: String, #[serde(default)] min: u32, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] format: Option<TextFormat> },
    /// After every statement and before commit, the indexed target point
    /// must exist for each row this transaction inserted (`after insert`).
    FinalExistsOnInsert { table: String, index: String, key: Vec<RuleTerm> },
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
pub enum TextFormat {
    Handle,
    Url,
    Email,
}

impl TextFormat {
    pub fn matches(self, text: &str) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Handle => text.bytes().all(|byte| byte.is_ascii_lowercase() || byte.is_ascii_digit() || byte == b'_'),
            Self::Url => text
                .strip_prefix("https://")
                .or_else(|| text.strip_prefix("http://"))
                .is_some_and(|rest| !rest.is_empty() && rest.chars().all(|c| !c.is_whitespace() && !c.is_control())),
            // @ref LLP 3000.013.001#1-decision — one deterministic ASCII mailbox grammar is shared by arguments, writes and devices.
            Self::Email => {
                if text.len() > 254 { return false; }
                let Some((local, domain)) = text.split_once('@') else { return false };
                if domain.contains('@') || local.is_empty() || local.len() > 64
                    || domain.is_empty() || domain.len() > 253
                    || local.starts_with('.') || local.ends_with('.') || local.contains("..")
                {
                    return false;
                }
                let local_ok = local.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric()
                    || matches!(b, b'.' | b'!' | b'#' | b'$' | b'%' | b'&' | b'\'' | b'*'
                        | b'+' | b'/' | b'=' | b'?' | b'^' | b'_' | b'`' | b'{' | b'|'
                        | b'}' | b'~' | b'-'));
                local_ok && domain.split('.').all(|label| !label.is_empty() && label.len() <= 63
                    && !label.starts_with('-') && !label.ends_with('-')
                    && label.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b == b'-'))
            }
        }
    }
}

//! Table names may change; numeric row, reference and receipt lifetimes do not.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#8-wave-21-release-evolution — renames are identity moves, never row copies or merges.
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
pub(super) const VERSION:&str="snapback4-release-store-9";
fn refuse(s:impl Into<String>)->RuntimeError {RuntimeError::Structural{code:"E_ADOPT",message:s.into()}}

// Only structural table identifiers change. Literal values, column names,
// operation names and authored defaults are never table identifiers.
fn identifiers(value:&mut serde_json::Value,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>) {
    match value {
        serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>for x in xs {identifiers(x,map)},
        serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>for (key,x) in fields {
            if key=="Literal" || key=="defaults" {continue;}
            if matches!(key.as_str(),"table"|"parent") {if let Some(name)=x.as_str().and_then(|s|map.get(s)){*x=serde_json::Value::String(name.clone());continue;}}
            identifiers(x,map);
        },_=>()
    }
}
fn mapped(name:&str,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->String {map.get(name).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||name.into())}
fn remap<T:serde::Serialize+serde::de::DeserializeOwned>(value:&T,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<T,RuntimeError> {
    let mut json=serde_json::to_value(value).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;identifiers(&mut json,map);
    serde_json::from_value(json).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))
}
pub(crate) fn schema(mut schema:Schema,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<Schema,RuntimeError> {
    if map.is_empty(){return Ok(schema)}
    schema.tables=std::mem::take(&mut schema.tables).into_iter().map(|(name,table)|Ok((mapped(&name,map),remap(&table,map)?))).collect::<Result<_,RuntimeError>>()?;
    for m in &mut schema.maintains {*m=remap(m,map)?;m.source=mapped(&m.source,map);m.target=mapped(&m.target,map);}
    for f in &mut schema.backfills {fill(f,map,false)?;}
    for f in &mut schema.downfills {fill(f,map,true)?;}
    for e in &mut schema.extractions {e.child=mapped(&e.child,map);}
    // Remaining table-bearing structural metadata is normalized through the
    // same typed codec; maintains' source/target are handled explicitly above.
    // These maps contain column identifiers, including arbitrary keys such as
    // "parent". Normalize their typed table slots, never their column strings.
    let mut extractions=std::mem::take(&mut schema.extractions);
    for e in &mut extractions {e.parent=mapped(&e.parent,map);}
    let columns=std::mem::take(&mut schema.asset_renames).into_iter().map(|(name,columns)|(mapped(&name,map),columns)).collect();
    schema.table_renames.clear();schema=remap(&schema,map)?;
    schema.extractions=extractions;schema.asset_renames=columns;
    Ok(schema)
}
fn fill(f:&mut crate::schema::backfill::Backfill,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>,down:bool)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    *f=remap(f,map)?;
    f.program.name=format!("{}{}{}",if down{"down"}else{"backfill"},f.table,f.name);Ok(())
}
pub(super) fn prepare(conn:&Connection,next:&Schema,catalog:&mut BTreeMap<String,i64>)->Result<BTreeMap<String,String>,RuntimeError> {
    let exists:bool=conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_table_renames')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;
    if !exists && next.table_renames.is_empty(){return Ok(BTreeMap::new())}
    conn.execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _table_renames(name TEXT PRIMARY KEY,origin TEXT NOT NULL) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    let mut names:BTreeMap<String,String>=conn.prepare("SELECT name,origin FROM _table_renames")?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
    for (new,old) in &next.table_renames {
        if new==old || !next.tables.contains_key(new) || next.tables.contains_key(old){return Err(refuse("table rename requires a distinct predecessor-only source"))}
        let origin=names.get(old).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||old.clone());
        if names.get(new).is_some_and(|root|root!=&origin){return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} conflicts with an independent retained identity")))}
        if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(catalog.get(old),catalog.get(new)) {if a!=b{return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} cannot merge existing table lifetimes")))}}
        for name in [old,new] {names.insert(name.clone(),origin.clone());conn.execute("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO _table_renames VALUES(?1,?2)",params![name,origin])?;}
    }
    let mut selected=BTreeMap::<String,String>::new();
    for name in next.tables.keys() {if let Some(root)=names.get(name) {if let Some(other)=selected.insert(root.clone(),name.clone()){return Err(refuse(format!("table aliases {other} and {name} cannot be active together")))}}}
    let map:BTreeMap<String,String>=names.iter().filter_map(|(name,root)|selected.get(root).filter(|new|*new!=name).map(|new|(name.clone(),new.clone()))).collect();
    for (old,new) in &map {
        if !catalog.contains_key(old){continue;}
        if catalog.contains_key(new){return Err(refuse(format!("table rename {old} → {new} conflicts with retained table storage")))}
        let entries:Vec<_>=catalog.iter().filter(|(key,_)|*key==old || key.starts_with(&format!("{old}."))).map(|(k,v)|(k.clone(),*v)).collect();
        for (key,num) in entries {let renamed=format!("{new}{}",&key[old.len()..]);conn.execute("UPDATE _catalog SET name=?1 WHERE name=?2",params![renamed,key])?;catalog.remove(&key);catalog.insert(renamed,num);}
        conn.execute("UPDATE _assets SET home_table=?1 WHERE home_table=?2",params![new,old])?;
        for table in ["_dormant_tables","_dormant_columns","_dormant_indexes"] {
            if conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {table} SET table_name=?1 WHERE table_name=?2"),params![new,old])?;}
        }
    }
    // Retained typed definitions must use the current public table names too.
    for table in ["_dormant_tables","_dormant_columns","_dormant_narrow_rows"] {
        if !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {continue;}
        let rows:Vec<(String,String)>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT definition,definition FROM {table} GROUP BY definition"))?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        for (old,text) in rows {let mut json:serde_json::Value=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;identifiers(&mut json,&map);let new=canonical_json(&json);if old!=new {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {table} SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2"),params![new,old])?;}}
    }
    history(conn,&map)?;
    conn.execute("UPDATE _meta SET value=?1 WHERE key='version'",[VERSION])?;
    Ok(map)
}
fn history(conn:&Connection,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<(),RuntimeError> {
    for (table,down) in [("_backfill_definitions",false),("_downfill_definitions",true)] {
        if !conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name=?1)",[table],|r|r.get::<_,bool>(0))? {continue;}
        let rows:Vec<(String,String)>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT digest,definition FROM {table}"))?.query_map([],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        for (old,text) in rows {let mut f:crate::schema::backfill::Backfill=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;fill(&mut f,map,down)?;let new=if down {f.down_key()}else{f.key()};if old==new{continue;}
            conn.execute(&format!("INSERT OR IGNORE INTO {table} VALUES(?1,?2)"),params![new,canonical_json(&f)])?;
            for ledger in ["_backfill_evidence","_backfill_pruned","_backfill_absence"] {conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {ledger} SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2"),params![new,old])?;}
            conn.execute("UPDATE _backfill_absence SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2",params![format!("$native:{new}"),format!("$native:{old}")])?;
            conn.execute("UPDATE _backfill_absence SET definition=?1 WHERE definition=?2",params![format!("$authored:{new}"),format!("$authored:{old}")])?;
            for c in f.inputs.keys() {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_refs SET column_name=?1 WHERE column_name=?2",params![format!("$backfill:{new}:{c}"),format!("$backfill:{old}:{c}")])?;}
            conn.execute(&format!("DELETE FROM {table} WHERE digest=?1"),[old])?;
        }
    }Ok(())
}

//! Asset/reference renames transport cells, homes and lifetime evidence.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.002#8-wave-21-release-evolution — asset identity is preserved; no upload, re-claim or generated placeholder.
use super::*;
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
type Maps=BTreeMap<String,BTreeMap<String,String>>;
fn refusal(message:impl Into<String>)->RuntimeError {RuntimeError::Structural{code:"E_ADOPT",message:message.into()}}
fn replace_keys<T>(fields:BTreeMap<String,T>,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->BTreeMap<String,T> {fields.into_iter().map(|(name,value)|(map.get(&name).cloned().unwrap_or(name),value)).collect()}
fn table(table:&mut Table,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>){
    table.columns=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.columns),map);
    table.defaults=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.defaults),map);
    table.column_reads=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.column_reads),map);
    table.on_delete=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.on_delete),map);
    table.written_by=replace_keys(std::mem::take(&mut table.written_by),map);
    fn term(t:&mut crate::schema::RuleTerm,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>){use crate::schema::RuleTerm::*;if let Field(c)|OldField(c)|NextField(c)|Reference{column:c,..}|ReferenceChain{column:c,..}=t {if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}}}
    fn rule(r:&mut crate::schema::RuleExpr,map:&BTreeMap<String,String>){use crate::schema::RuleExpr::*;match r {Eq(a,b)|Ne(a,b)|Compare(_,a,b)=>{term(a,map);term(b,map)},And(xs)|Or(xs)=>for x in xs{rule(x,map)},Not(r)=>rule(r,map),Exists{key,equalities,..}|Created{key,equalities,..}=>{for t in key{term(t,map)}for(_,t)in equalities{term(t,map)}},_=>()}}
    for r in [&mut table.rules.read,&mut table.rules.insert,&mut table.rules.update,&mut table.rules.delete]{rule(r,map)}
    for r in table.column_reads.values_mut(){rule(r,map)}
    for index in table.indexes.values_mut(){for component in &mut index.components {if let crate::schema::IndexComponent::Column(c)=component {if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}}}}
    if let Some(sync)=&mut table.sync {
        for audience in std::iter::once(&mut sync.audience).chain(sync.also.iter_mut().map(|a|&mut a.audience)) {
            match audience {crate::schema::Audience::Column(c)=>{if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}},crate::schema::Audience::Target{prefix,..}=>for c in prefix {if let Some(new)=map.get(c){*c=new.clone();}},_=>()}
        }
    }
    for c in &mut table.constraints {match c{
        crate::schema::Constraint::Immutable{fields}|crate::schema::Constraint::Cap{by:fields,..}=>for f in fields{if let Some(new)=map.get(f){*f=new.clone();}},
        crate::schema::Constraint::FinalExistsOnInsert{key,..}=>for t in key{term(t,map)},
        _=>(),
    }}
}

pub(super) fn schema(mut schema:Schema,maps:&Maps)->Schema {

    for e in &mut schema.extractions {
        if let Some(map)=maps.get(&e.child){e.reference=map.get(&e.reference).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||e.reference.clone());e.fields=std::mem::take(&mut e.fields).into_iter().map(|(dest,source)|(map.get(&dest).cloned().unwrap_or(dest),source)).collect();}
        if let Some(map)=maps.get(&e.parent){for source in e.fields.values_mut(){if let Some(new)=map.get(source){*source=new.clone();}}}
    }schema
}
pub(super) fn prepare(conn:&Connection,next:&Schema,catalog:&BTreeMap<String,i64>,renamed_tables:&BTreeMap<String,String>)->Result<Maps,RuntimeError>{
    let exists:bool=conn.query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM sqlite_schema WHERE name='_asset_column_renames')",[],|r|r.get(0))?;
    if !exists && next.asset_renames.is_empty(){return Ok(Maps::new())}
    conn.execute_batch("CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS _asset_column_renames(t INTEGER NOT NULL,name TEXT NOT NULL,origin TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(t,name)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
    let mut maps=Maps::new();
    for (name,current) in &next.tables {
        let Some(t)=catalog.get(name) else {continue};
        let mut aliases:BTreeMap<String,String>=conn.prepare("SELECT name,origin FROM _asset_column_renames WHERE t=?1")?.query_map([t],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?)))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
        for (new,old) in next.asset_renames.get(name).into_iter().flatten() {
            if old==new || current.columns.contains_key(old) || !current.columns.get(new).is_some_and(asset){return Err(refusal(format!("asset rename {name}.{old} → {new} requires one predecessor-only source and one asset destination")))}
            let root=aliases.get(old).cloned().unwrap_or_else(||old.clone());

        }
        let mut selected=BTreeMap::new();for c in current.columns.keys(){if let Some(root)=aliases.get(c){if selected.insert(root.clone(),c.clone()).is_some(){return Err(refusal("two names for the same asset column cannot be active together"))}}}
        let map:BTreeMap<_,_>=aliases.into_iter().filter_map(|(old,root)|selected.get(&root).filter(|new|**new!=old).map(|new|(old,new.clone()))).collect();
        for(old,new)in &map {
            let prior:Option<String>=conn.query_row("SELECT schema FROM _generations WHERE generation=(SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='generation')",[],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
            if let Some(prior)=prior {

                let prior=prior.with_table_names(renamed_tables)?;
                if let Some(before)=prior.table(name).filter(|t|t.columns.contains_key(old)){
                    let source=&before.columns[old];let destination=&current.columns[new];
                    if reference(source)||reference(destination){
                        if source!=destination||before.on_delete.get(old)!=current.on_delete.get(new)||before.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t|t.reference==*old)||current.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t|t.reference==*new){return Err(refusal("reference rename requires identical nominal type, deletion policy and uncaptured ownership in both directions"))}
                    }
                }
            }
            for rows in ["r","_dormant_rows","_dormant_narrow_rows"] {
                if !exists_table(conn,rows)?{continue;}
                let a=format!("$.Row.{}",serde_json::to_string(old).unwrap());let b=format!("$.Row.{}",serde_json::to_string(new).unwrap());
                let conflict:bool=conn.query_row(&format!("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM {rows} WHERE t=?1 AND json_type(data,?2) IS NOT NULL AND json_type(data,?3) IS NOT NULL AND json_extract(data,?2)!=json_extract(data,?3))"),params![t,a,b],|r|r.get(0))?;
                if conflict{return Err(refusal(format!("asset rename {name}.{old} → {new} has a conflicting physical destination")))}
                conn.execute(&format!("UPDATE {rows} SET data=json_remove(json_set(data,?3,json_extract(data,?2)),?2) WHERE t=?1 AND json_type(data,?2) IS NOT NULL"),params![t,a,b])?;
            }
            conn.execute("UPDATE _assets SET home_column=?3 WHERE home_table=?1 AND home_column=?2",params![name,old,new])?;
            if exists_table(conn,"_dormant_refs")? {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_refs SET column_name=?3 WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2",params![t,old,new])?;}
            if exists_table(conn,"_dormant_values")? {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_values SET column_name=?3 WHERE t=?1 AND column_name=?2",params![t,old,new])?;}
            if exists_table(conn,"_dormant_columns")? {conn.execute("UPDATE _dormant_columns SET column_name=?3 WHERE table_name=?1 AND column_name=?2",params![name,old,new])?;}
        }
        if !map.is_empty(){
            for rows in ["_dormant_narrow_rows","_dormant_tables"] {
                if !exists_table(conn,rows)?{continue;}
                let condition=if rows=="_dormant_tables"{"table_name=?1"}else{"t=?1"};

                let definitions:Vec<String>=conn.prepare(&format!("SELECT DISTINCT definition FROM {rows} WHERE {condition}"))?.query_map([&value],|r|r.get(0))?.collect::<Result<_,_>>()?;
